- **Tests:** fixtures of both formats; two-identical-reads rule for Handy; WAL copy consistency.
- **Exit criteria:** import works on the owner's Mac with a real Wispr Flow install.
- **Effort / depends on:** S / S-16, S-17.

### S-36 Vibe coding

- **Goal:** Wispr's vibe-coding helpers for VS Code, Cursor and Windsurf, locally.
- **User-visible outcome:** Settings › Vibe coding: "Recognize code names · Set up" walks the user through turning on the IDE's screen-reader-optimized mode, after which identifiers visible in the editor are used as spelling hints (camelCase, snake_case kept); "Tag files in chat" (toggle): in Cursor/Windsurf chat inputs, "index dot ts" becomes `@index.ts` when a matching file name is visible (open tabs or explorer via AX). Off until set up.
- **R2 rows closed:** D-59, D-60
- **R4 parts:** S-20 context layer; Wispr docs 6434410694 (behaviour).
- **New fork files:** `fork/context/ide.rs`, `fork/text/file_tags.rs`, `src/fork/settings/VibeCoding.tsx`.
- **Tests:** identifier extraction; file-tag rewrite only in listed apps and only for visible names.
- **Real-Mac acceptance:** dictate "rename get user by id" in Cursor with the identifier visible.
- **Exit criteria:** no filesystem scanning; works with AX only.
- **Effort / depends on:** L / S-20, S-19a.
- **As built (2026-11-02):** `fork/context/ide.rs` (editor ids for VS Code/Insiders, Cursor, Windsurf; per-editor access; chat-input detection by labels; exact camelCase/PascalCase/snake_case extraction, ≤ 20 per take; spoken-form → spelling stage `identifiers`) and `fork/text/file_tags.rs` (visible file names from AX tab/explorer rows, ≤ 40; spoken "x dot y" or written `x.y` → `@x.y` only for a visible name, stage `file_tags`, re-run after the LLM) ride S-20's single AX read (`caret.rs` `IdeScan`, its own 8,000-character/1,500-element budget inside the same time budget); only names leave the read, the log has counts and the editor id. Code names lead S-20's hints, so the recognizer vocabulary, spelling stage and cleanup prompt use them too. `src/fork/settings/VibeCoding.tsx` adds Settings › Vibe coding (setup: editors, three steps to set Editor: Accessibility Support on, confirmation; then the switch, Change editors, and Tag files in chat). No Handy file, no migration; `vibe_coding.file_tags_enabled` (serde default, settings stay v6) and validation (known editors, each once; nothing on before the setup). **Decisions:** (1) the setup is confirmed by the user, since checking an editor's setting would mean reading its files; (2) file tags also need the setup and that editor listed (DEC-41), although tabs are readable without screen-reader mode; (3) a chat input is recognized only by its labels (chat/composer/cascade/agent or "@ to mention"), never fail-open; (4) a spoken form two visible identifiers or file names share is left alone; (5) tagging after the LLM runs in `after_llm`, not in `deliver`, so snippet bodies stay as saved; (6) vibe coding only adds to a read context awareness allows (the page says so when it is off). Gates after the rebase onto F-02: 1,399 Rust tests passed/32 ignored, fork clippy 0, Playwright fork suite 330 passed/10 skipped on installed Chrome (new `vibe-coding.spec.ts`: setup flow, file-tag gating, keyboard-only, RTL, axe light/dark; Settings sweeps include the page), ESLint, tsc, translation parity, guards and formatting passed. The 22 new strings are copy-English/`untranslated` in 25 locales pending the wave batch. Two timing tests (`ten_thousand_rows_search_within_100_ms`, `full_profile_is_fast_enough`) failed once at load average 45 from parallel builders; the voice one now takes best of 3 like the search test, both pass. **Mac wave 1 (2026-11-02):** S-36.5 (TextEdit not tagged) and S-36.7 PASS; the Cursor editor take read 1 code name in 34 characters with no respelling (`identifiers=0`) and a fresh Cursor profile puts a Log In window over the editor, so S-36.1-.4 stay open; `fs_usage` needs sudo (`docs/fork/test-runs/1.0.0-alpha.1-M-26-macwave-1.md`). **Not run (queued for the S-19b + S-36 Mac wave, `scripts/wb-s36/`, MAC-TEST-SCRIPT S-36.1-.8):** the real Cursor take "rename get user by id", the chat tag, the off/unset/TextEdit negatives, `fs_usage` proof of no filesystem access, read latency in Electron trees, and whether Cursor's tab/explorer rows and chat-input labels match the roles and words assumed here. VS Code and Windsurf are not installed: unit tests only.
- **Mac wave 1 follow-up (2026-11-03, `fix/wave1-b`):** S-36.1's `identifiers=0` points at the driver, not the scan (inferred from the log and the script; not yet confirmed on the Mac). `scripts/wb-s36/take.sh` rewrote the throwaway profile's `settings.json` but never removed the profile, so Cursor restored the earlier `chat` take's window and tabs: the read saw 34 characters and one code name, which fits `index.ts` (`export * from "./user_service";`, whose only code name is `user_service`) and not `user_service.ts`, the take went into `index.ts`, and the saved `user_service.ts` never held it. The editor scan itself matches VS Code/Cursor's tree: Monaco's view lines are `aria-hidden`, so the screen-reader `AXTextArea` (caret window) is the editor text, and tabs (`AXRadioButton`/`AXTabButton`) and explorer rows (`AXRow`) are walked from its ancestors. Fix: the driver removes the profile on every take (or copies a prepared one from `WB_S36_PROFILE`) and sets `window.restoreWindows: none`. No recorded Cursor AX fixture: an unsigned-in throwaway Cursor exposed only 12 empty `AXGroup` nodes within 14 s (the Electron tree was not built yet), so the queued Mac rerun is the evidence.

### S-37 Accessibility, localization and copy pass

- **Goal:** meet §5.5 and §5.6 everywhere, and confirm all copy is our own.
- **User-visible outcome:** VoiceOver reads the Hub, settings, onboarding and the pill; keyboard-only use works; all 26 languages complete (web and Rust strings).
- **R2 rows closed:** E-81
- **Regression protected (HAVE):** B-68, E-80
- **R4 parts:** eslint-plugin-jsx-a11y 6.10.2 and @axe-core/playwright (MIT / MPL-2.0 dev only, DEC-36); i18next-cli 1.80.5 (MIT, dev; never locize commands); `scripts/wb-translate-missing.ts` (W-25, from F-05).
- **New fork files:** `fork/pill/accessibility.rs` (NSAccessibility announcements), `docs/fork/VOICEOVER-CHECKLIST.md` (scripted steps per screen: focus order, every control's spoken name and state, announcements), `scripts/wb-copy-check.ts` (compares English fork strings with R1 quotes, §1), `ci/i18n-strict`.
- **Tests:** axe on every page (0 serious/critical); RTL snapshots (ar, he); key parity and placeholder checks; strict i18n mode on (no `machine`/`untranslated` flags left); copy check green.
- **Real-Mac acceptance:** the VoiceOver checklist passes for onboarding, the Hub, settings and one dictation; Increase Contrast and Reduce Motion on.
- **Exit criteria:** §5.5 checklist passes; no flagged fork key in any locale; human review done for the 10 sample-text languages.
- **Effort / depends on:** M / all UI slices.
- **As built, part a: accessibility and copy (S-37a, 2026-11-03, `slice/s37a`; the localization half, machine translation review, plural forms and `ci/i18n-strict`, is S-37b, built in parallel)** (details in `docs/fork/ARCHITECTURE.md` "Accessibility and copy pass (S-37a)"): (1) **`fork/pill/accessibility.rs`** now holds every VoiceOver announcement: the S-03 `announcement` table (moved from `pill/mod.rs`), the application-element post (moved from `panel.rs`/`native.rs`), `spoken` (the key localized through `fork/i18n.rs`) and two completions of §5.5: **inserted** ("Text inserted" after a pasted take, `insert::publish_outcome` → the pill controller, so it uses the bar's language and never runs where the bar does not) and **card text** (the card's reason, then its words cut at 280 characters on a word; a password field's card has no text in the view, so none is spoken). S-30's spotlight hint posts through the same function. (2) **`tests/fork/a11y-sweep.spec.ts`**: axe (WCAG 2.1 AA, 0 serious/critical) on every Hub page, every Settings section with Handy's hosted rows **no longer excluded**, the Hub dialogs and popovers and every onboarding step, each in light, dark and `prefers-contrast: more` (both themes), always with Reduce Motion on and a check that nothing keeps animating; a keyboard-only walk (Tab through every Hub page, every Settings section and onboarding's first screen: every stop named and with a visible ring; Enter on a sidebar item; Esc closes Settings). The shared `expectNoSeriousAxeFindings` no longer excludes `[data-handy-content]` either. (3) **Findings fixed in product code:** Reduce Motion: spinners (`animate-spin` in seven dialogs and the app picker) kept turning; a global rule in `tokens.css` ends every animation and transition at once (end events still fire). Focus: the shared `TabPanel` had `outline-none` with the focus ring classes, which in Tailwind 4 removes the ring (Dictionary and Style tab panels showed none). Handy's hosted rows (Handy's files unchanged, R5 §4.2): the output device's reset button had no name, the history-limit number field and the post-processing model select had no label, the language search field set `aria-expanded` without a role, its text fields drew no focus ring and a disabled row's title at 50 % opacity failed contrast; `src/fork/settings/handyA11y.ts` (one hook in `SettingsModal`) names Handy's unnamed controls from their row title ("Reset Output Device"), gives the search field `role="combobox"`, and `tokens.css` draws the ring and raises disabled-row text to 75 %. (4) **Increase Contrast:** `@media (prefers-contrast: more)` in `tokens.css` retunes the secondary text, lines, fills, accent and tags in both themes and thickens focus rings to 3 px; the sweep runs axe with it on. (5) **`scripts/wb-copy-check.ts`** (`bun run wb:copy-check`, a step in `wb-ci.yml`, unit tests in `wb-copy-check.test.ts`): R1's double-quoted Wispr strings of five or more words plus RV-24's list against every English fork string (`en/fork.json` without Handy's rebranded `overrides`, and the English sample texts); a shared run of five or more words, after folding case, punctuation, ⌥/Opt and the product name (Flow / Wispr / Wispr Bro / `{{appName}}`), fails; reviewed exceptions would go in `wb-copy-check.allow.json` (none needed). One match found and rewritten: onboarding's practice title "Practice: write an email hands-free" (R1: "Write an email hands free") → "Practice: a whole email, hands-free". (6) **`docs/fork/VOICEOVER-CHECKLIST.md`**: the scripted by-ear pass (onboarding, every Hub page, Settings, the Scratchpad, lessons, the bar and one dictation; ★ lines again with Increase Contrast and Reduce Motion). (7) **`scripts/wb-s37/{axtree.swift,run.sh}`**: the AX-tree check for the running app (every control's AXRole/AXTitle/AXDescription/AXTitleUIElement, fails on an unnamed one), driven through the screens with S-31's `lesson.swift`; MAC-TEST-SCRIPT S-37.3. **New English strings (for S-37b):** `rust.pill.announce.inserted`, `settingsModal.resetSetting`; **changed:** `onboardingFlow.practice.title` (the 25 other locales still hold the old sentence's translation). `package.json` +1 line (`wb:copy-check`, budget 47). **Gates** (on `wispr-bro` 91027e8c; rebased onto 735d25b2, Mac wave 2 driver and doc changes only, then guards, lint, formatting, translations and the copy check re-run): 1,597 Rust tests passed/33 ignored, fork clippy 0, `cargo fmt`, ESLint, tsc, both translation checks, guards, `wb:format:check`, `wb:copy-check`, 651 Playwright passed/10 skipped on installed Chrome (3 workers; the a11y sweep is 59 tests, ~8 min); `bun test scripts/` fails only the two in-process "current tree" guard tests that fail on `wispr-bro` too (S-38 part 2). With 5 workers at load ~96 (other agents) six pill/recovery tests timed out opening a page; all passed rerun at 2 workers, so they are load-only and were not changed. **Not verified:** VoiceOver by ear and the AX-tree run on the Mac (S-37.1-.3, queued for the Mac wave); WebKit's own `prefers-contrast` mapping of Increase Contrast (Chromium emulation only); the Scratchpad, pill and spotlight pages are covered by their own specs' axe, not re-run with `prefers-contrast: more`.
- **As built, part b: localization preparation (S-37b, 2026-11-03, `slice/s37b`)** (details in `docs/fork/ARCHITECTURE.md` "i18n and the untranslated marking scheme", reviewer guide `docs/fork/TRANSLATION-REVIEW.md`). Part a (accessibility, VoiceOver checklist, copy check) is S-37a, built in parallel. (1) **Plurals.** Every fork string that shows a count is an i18next plural group: English `_one`/`_other` (checked: exactly those two); every other locale exactly its CLDR cardinal categories from `Intl.PluralRules(lang).resolvedOptions().pluralCategories` (`ja/ko/zh/zh-TW/vi/id` `_other` only, `ru/uk/pl` `_one/_few/_many/_other`, `cs` + `_few/_many`, `he` `_two`, `ar` all six, `ca/es/fr/it/pt` a `_many` for millions). Newly converted (with `count` at the call site): voice-profile progress/locked/locked title and body, the dictionary and snippet length problems, snippet import limits, cleanup "N of M paragraphs", the typing-test instruction, time-saved basis, hotkeys "up to N shortcuts", transform examples help, the import preview caption and Insights footnote (`_one` added), and the Rust milestone notifications (`milestoneWords`, `milestoneStreak`, now `count` args). `aiEngine.apple.counts` (three numbers) became label-value ("requests 12, declined 1, …") instead of a plural. Decision: a message with two numbers pluralizes on `count`; the other number is a fixed constant. Decision: old inbox rows from dev profiles with the pre-plural milestone keys show the key (nothing is released). (2) **Rust.** `fork::i18n::t_plural`/`plural_category` on ICU4X `icu_plurals` 2.1 (Unicode-3.0, compiled CLDR data, same family as `icu_normalizer`; new crates `icu_plurals`, `icu_plurals_data`, `icu_locale`, `icu_locale_data`, `fixed_decimal`, THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES regenerated): the app menu's counts and macOS notifications (`notify::render` re-resolves the stored `_one/_other` key per locale, as the inbox already did with i18next). `rust.*` strings live in the same `fork.json`/`fork.flags.json`, so the parity, placeholder, plural, flag and strict checks cover tray, menu, notification and VoiceOver strings; a Rust test checks that ICU4X and the browser's CLDR agree on every locale's forms. (3) **Script** (`wb-translate-missing.ts`): plural-aware parity and `--check`; a locale's `_one` is translated from English `_one` (the singular) unless that lacks a placeholder `_other` has (Russian "one" also covers 21), every other form from `_other`, with category and example counts as a hint; placeholders now compare `{{name, format}}` (the old pattern skipped `{{count, number}}` and `{{x y}}`, which hid broken machine strings); never-reviewed entries with wrong placeholders, English copies and machine strings that lost a kept name ("Polish" as the language, "Fn") or mix in another language's script (131 Hebrew strings had Arabic, Cyrillic or Thai letters) are translated again; a changed English string is noticed (hashes in `en/fork.source-hashes.json`) and translated again in every locale (S-37a's new `onboardingFlow.practice.title` was re-translated by hand-dropping it once, since the hash file starts with this slice); letterless strings are not flagged; `--locale`, `--review <lang>` (worklist TSV). The `llama-server` prompt now carries the locale's tone and glossary (`scripts/wb-translate-glossary.ts`). (4) **Machine translation wave** with the pinned b11624 sidecar and Qwen3-4B-Instruct-2507 Q4_K_M (`nice -n 10`), then hand fixes of obvious errors (the `_few/_many` forms of the `rust.menu` counts and Rust notifications in ru/uk/pl/cs, this slice's German strings); flags stay `machine` for review. Hebrew needed hand translation for 30 strings the 4B model kept writing with Arabic/Cyrillic/Thai letters; a few more hand fixes elsewhere (lost "Polish", transliterated "Wispr Bro"). **Flags left after the wave** (fork strings `machine` / `untranslated`, plus brand `overrides.*` `machine`): ar 2196/0 (+33), bg 1848/0, ca 1935/0, cs 2022/0, da 1848/0, de 1848/0, es 1935/0, fr 1935/0, he 1935/0, hi 1848/0, id 1761/0, it 1935/0, ja 1761/0, ko 1761/0, ne 1848/0, nl 1848/0 (+34), pl 2022/0, pt 1935/0, ru 2022/0, sv 1848/0, tr 1848/0, uk 2022/0 (+32), vi 1761/0, zh 1762/0, zh-TW 1763/0 (+33 overrides unless noted); 0 `untranslated` anywhere, including S-37a's `rust.pill.announce.inserted`, `settingsModal.resetSetting` and the reworded `onboardingFlow.practice.title`. (5) **`ci/i18n-strict`**: `--strict` fails on any `untranslated`/`machine` flag (brand `overrides.*` included); CI step "Strict i18n" runs `wb:check:translations:strict` (`--strict-switch`), enforced only with `I18N_STRICT=on` in `ci/i18n-strict`, now **off** until the review agents clear the flags. (6) **`docs/fork/TRANSLATION-REVIEW.md`**: procedure to clear a flag, tone per locale (macOS's form of address), glossary per locale for 14 terms (Apple's macOS terms where they exist), plural table; a test keeps it equal to the prompt's glossary. **Gates** after rebasing onto `wispr-bro` 9055ac21 (S-37a and the Mac wave 2 fixes merged): 1,609 Rust tests passed/33 ignored, fork clippy 0, `cargo fmt`, ESLint, tsc, both translation checks, guards, `wb:format:check`, `bun test scripts/` 282/0, 651 Playwright passed/10 skipped on installed Chrome (the inbox keeps "10,000" grouped in plural titles: it now resolves the form with `Intl.PluralRules` itself). **Queued for the Mac wave:** S-37b.1 menu counts in ru and ar/pl (`scripts/wb-s37b/plurals.sh`), S-37b.2 a Russian notification's plural form. **Not done here (review step):** clearing `machine` flags; the 4B model's quality is low (wrong forms, mixed du/Sie, mistranslated feature names), so every machine string needs the review.
- **As built, part c: AI review, English copy pass and strict on (2026-11-03, `i18n/g1`..`g7`, `i18n/english`).** (1) **AI review.** Seven review agents read every fork string (`rust.*` and brand `overrides.*` included) in all 25 locales against the glossary, tone and plural rules of `docs/fork/TRANSLATION-REVIEW.md` and flagged each one `ai-reviewed` (it passes strict mode and stays visible until a person clears it). **This is an AI review, not a native speaker's:** no person has read the translations. (2) **English copy pass** (`fix(i18n)` commit): the reviewers' list of doubtful English source strings, checked against the call sites. Mac key names (Return, Esc; the Command Mode card shows its real binding instead of a fixed "fn ⌃", and its help no longer names the keys), "shortcut" not "hotkey", US spelling (canceled, favorite), "last dictation" in the tray and app menu, "Hold to talk", the app menu's Dictionary (was Vocabulary), "this Mac" (also in Handy's microphone and login-item rows via overrides), "menu bar" in Handy's tray-icon setting (Handy's `onboarding.done.subTray` keeps "system tray": it only shows on Windows/Linux), clearer import, snippet, whisper-lesson and update-policy sentences, the take-recovered notification body as a plural group (Rust picks it by `count`), `history.resultsMore`, the examples read error with `{{chars}}`, the flag dialog's dictionary limit messages now get `count` (they showed the key), and the unused `hub.pages.*.empty` placeholders and `transforms.commandMode.shortcut` deleted. (3) **Retranslation:** every changed key was re-translated in all 25 locales by AI reviewers (flag `ai-reviewed`): 1,458 fork strings (ar 90; cs, pl, ru, uk 72; ca, es, fr, he, it, pt 63; bg, da, de, hi, ne, nl, sv, tr 54; id, ja, ko, vi, zh, zh-TW 45) plus 3 brand overrides per locale; nl's `overrides.settings.general.shortcut.title` now uses the glossary term ("Toetscombinaties"). A check with OpenCC found 452 zh strings that the earlier zh review had left in Traditional characters and Taiwan vocabulary; they were rewritten in mainland Simplified Chinese (`i18n(zh)` commit), and `scripts/wb-translate-glossary.ts` took the zh/zh-TW tone lines the review had written into the guide (its test failed on `wispr-bro`). (4) **Strict on:** `ci/i18n-strict` says `I18N_STRICT=on`; `bun scripts/wb-translate-missing.ts --strict` passes for all 25 locales (0 `untranslated`, 0 `machine`). From now on a branch that adds or changes English translates it in every locale before merging (TRANSLATION-REVIEW.md). The release-notes draft in `wb-release.yml` says the translations were reviewed by AI, not by native speakers. **Still open (exit criterion):** the human review of the 10 sample-text languages (en, es, fr, de, pt, it, ja, zh, ko, ru), for the owner or the community; a person clears the `ai-reviewed` flags they have checked.
- **Mac wave 3 fix (2026-11-03, `fix/wave3`):** S-37.3's unnamed `AXIncrementor` was WebKit's spin buttons on Handy's History Limit `<input type="number">`; no ARIA attribute on the input names them, so `settings/handyA11y.ts` marks Handy's number fields (`data-wb-no-spinner`) and `tokens.css` hides their spin buttons. The field keeps its row's name and steps with ↑/↓. Handy's file untouched; no new strings. The a11y sweep checks every hosted number field. **Queued:** S-37.4.

### S-38 Performance and reliability hardening

- **Goal:** meet every §5 budget on the reference machines.
- **User-visible outcome:** fast, quiet, stable app.
- **R2 rows closed:** E-83
- **Regression protected (HAVE):** E-85
- **R4 parts:** none new; uses RS-03/RS-06/RS-09 harnesses.
- **Soak harness (`scripts/wb-soak/`):** a test-only virtual audio input (BlackHole, GPL-3.0, test machines only, never shipped) set as the app's microphone plays prerecorded WAV takes (incl. multi-line and snippet takes); each take is started and stopped with `--toggle-transcription` (the CLI goes through the same coordinator and `deliver` path as the hotkey); the driver focuses the target app and field by script before each take, then reads the field back through AX and compares it with the expected text; per take it logs outcome (`confirmed`/`card`/`lost`/`duplicate`/`wrong target`), latency and clipboard restore. A separate **hardware run** does 50 takes with a real fn key on a real keyboard, the tester following a printed script, results logged the same way.
- **New fork files:** `scripts/wb-soak/` (driver, AX read-back, per-app field scripts), `docs/fork/PERF.md` (measurements).
- **Tests:** latency benches (p50/p95 per §5.1), 8 h idle run, 500-take soak, 50-take hardware run, memory-pressure test on 8 GB, startup timing, single instance (E-85).
- **Exit criteria:** all §5 numbers met or the model tier changed to meet them (recorded).
- **Effort / depends on:** M / S-01..S-32.
- **As built, part 1 (2026-11-02; slices merged up to S-24 and F-02)** (details in `docs/fork/ARCHITECTURE.md` "Performance and reliability hardening, part 1 (S-38)", numbers in `docs/fork/PERF.md`, checks in `docs/fork/MAC-TEST-SCRIPT.md` S-38.2-S-38.4): (1) **§5.2 idle RSS: hidden windows are parked** (`fork/hub/park.rs`). The S-03 "As built" options were closing the Hub's web view when hidden or measuring footprint; the budget stays RSS and the web view goes: `main` and `fork-scratchpad`, hidden (closed) for 60 s, not minimized, and for `main` after its page reported ready (Handy's `App.tsx` initializes Enigo and the shortcuts from the page), are destroyed. `main` is rebuilt with Handy's builder settings, the S-13 chrome, Handy's appearance and its last frame by `ensure_main`, the first line of Handy's `show_main_window` (one `lib.rs` hook, §4.2 table) and of the fork's show paths; events for the new page (notification Open, View changes) wait for `fork_hub_ready`, which `useNotificationPageOpen` sends once its listeners are up. The Scratchpad was already rebuilt by its own `open`. `ExitRequested` without a code (every Tauri window gone) is refused while the Hub is parked; the tray glyph takes its theme from Handy's setting or the macOS appearance while there is no main window. macOS only. Debug build, same profile and Mac: total RSS at rest 383 MB with parking off → 216 MB parked (Hub WebContent and WebKit GPU gone, −167 MB); the release total is expected near 150 MB but is **not measured yet** (S-38.2). (2) **Flaky tests fixed, not re-run:** the S-09 WAL concurrency test (readers now open before a barrier; a fresh reader's first WAL-index read could hold the writer past its 5 s busy wait under full-suite load, 1 in ~20 loaded runs), Handy's clipboard stdio test (1 s budget vs a 3 s child; now 20 s vs 60 s, child killed; `ci/hooks.txt`), three wall-clock speed bounds (now thread CPU time, `testutil::CpuTimer`), the pill-page RTL tests (they set `dir` and raced the page's own language effect; now an RTL app language and a wait for the page's `dir`) and the pill axe test (one 48-load test over its 120 s budget; now one test per state and theme). One notification test now reports the page ready first. (3) The fork dependency block moved from `[target.'cfg(unix)'.dependencies]` to `[dependencies]`. (4) `scripts/wb-soak/`: `idle.py` (§5.2 sampler and verdict), `soak.py` + `play.swift` + `axread.swift` (the exactly-once driver as specified above; reuses `wb-s05/clip.swift` and `wb-s03-pill.swift`). (5) `docs/fork/PERF.md` started. (6) The bundle's executable is `Contents/MacOS/wispr-bro` (`mainBinaryName` in the overlay; crate and lib keep Handy's names, bundle id unchanged, so TCC grants stay; `.ips` pickup already matches by bundle id; drivers read `CFBundleExecutable`). An installed login item that points at the old `…/MacOS/handy` path would need re-registering, but nothing is released yet. **Not done in part 1:** the 500-take soak, the hardware run, latency benches, the 8 h run, startup timing and the 8 GB pressure test (part 2, reference machines); S-16's accent-folded word matching candidate (Siobhán) stays open. **Not verified:** the release-build idle numbers and the by-hand reopen paths (tray, Dock, notification, ⌥O, Scratchpad, minimize), queued as S-38.2/S-38.3; the soak needs BlackHole (S-38.4). **Mac wave 1 fix (`fix/wave1-a`, 2026-11-03):** the drivers no longer assume `./handy`: `scripts/wb-lib/app.sh` (`wb_bin_name`: `CFBundleExecutable` for a bundle, else the newest of `wispr-bro`/`handy`; `WB_BIN_RE` for `pgrep`; `wb_cancel`: `--cancel` only to a running instance, with a 15 s timeout, since with no instance it starts a whole app, the 9-minute hang) is used by the S-05/S-06/S-07a/S-07b/S-11/S-12a/S-12b/S-19a/S-19b/S-20/S-34/S-36 drivers and `wb-s09-soak.sh`; `wb-perf/startup.py` does the same; `scripts/wb-lib/app.test.ts` guards it.
- **As built, part 2 (2026-11-02; slices merged up to S-26b, S-28, S-29b and S-34; S-02 in parallel, its tap health untouched)** (details in `docs/fork/ARCHITECTURE.md` "Performance and reliability hardening, part 2 (S-38)", numbers in `docs/fork/PERF.md`, checks S-38.6-S-38.10): (1) **Accent-folded dictionary words** (S-16 finding closed): after Handy's stages, `fork::text::dictionary_accent_stage` runs S-20's spelling pass with the dictionary's active words that have letters outside ASCII and an ASCII accent-free key, so "Siobhan"/"siobhan" → "Siobhán", "cafe" → "café" (a sentence-initial "Cafe" → "Café"), never touching dictionary rule outputs; done in the fork stage, no Handy hook (Handy's `apply_custom_words` stays ASCII-only); CJK/Cyrillic words stay with the recognizer's vocabulary as in Handy. New stage name `dictionary_accents` in `entry_meta.text_stages`. (2) **Memory rule, §5.2 / risk #6:** one pure decision `memory::allows(running, snapshot, resident)` for cleanup, transforms, prewarm and warm-up: macOS critical pressure (`kern.memorystatus_vm_pressure_level` 4) → no LLM even when running, and the idle owner stops an idle server at once (`Supervisor::stop_if_idle`, never mid-request); a running server otherwise passes (decision: warn pressure changes nothing, the server's memory is already paid and the 6 s deadline fails a slow reply open); a stopped one needs model + 1.5 GB (unchanged). Debug builds simulate another Mac with `WB_DEBUG_MEMORY=physical_gb=8,available_mb=…,pressure=…` (LLM tier, headroom, pressure and the speech-model RAM tier). The 8 GB tier is unchanged: RS-06's combined RSS with Parakeet 0.6B was 2,873 MiB (≤ 3.2 GB) and the speech tier caps footprint at 1,400 MB; the real M1 8 GB run decides (S-38.7). (3) **§5.1 latency bench** (`fork/perf_bench.rs`, `scripts/wb-perf/{takes.ts,latency.sh,latency.ts}`, `eval/perf/takes.jsonl`): headless, shipped recognizer/pipeline/cleanup/Polish on the real sidecar, TTS takes written to files, PASS/FAIL per row (paste added from S-04's measured 41.7 ms p95). (4) **§5.3 startup timing:** `fork/startup.rs` logs `startup: ready N ms after launch (hotkeys …, bar …)` from the kernel's process start; `scripts/wb-perf/startup.py` runs N launches of a portable profile and prints PASS/FAIL. (5) **Hardware run:** `soak.py --hardware` (a person's fn take per printed sentence, word-level outcome). (6) **E-85:** a source test keeps the single-instance plugin and its CLI routes. **Reliability review against the merged slices:** offline/update silence (S-28), exactly-once and clipboard (S-04 soak), never-lose-words (S-07c/S-08/S-09), sidecar crash fail-open (S-10), negative cases (owned by S-04/S-05/S-07a/S-08/S-09) have their tests on `wispr-bro`; nothing cheap was left open except the items above. **Not done (need a person, BlackHole or other hardware), queued:** the 500-take soak (S-38.4, BlackHole), the 50-take hardware run (S-38.9, owner), the 8 h idle run (S-38.10), latency and startup on M1 8 GB / M-series 16 GB / Intel (S-38.6, S-38.8), the real 8 GB memory run (S-38.7). **Gates** after rebasing onto `wispr-bro` 5bf94cc7 (S-02 merged): 1,569 Rust tests passed/33 ignored, 527 Playwright passed/10 skipped (installed Chrome), fork clippy 0, fmt, ESLint, tsc, both translation checks, guards, `wb:format:check`; `bun test scripts/` fixed two stale expectations (NOTICE's es-toolkit, RS-09's quant candidates) that failed on `wispr-bro` too; the two in-process "current tree" tests of `wb-guards.test.ts` still fail on `wispr-bro` and here (`git ls-files` fails only inside that test file; the guard CLI passes), not fixed. No new strings, command or migration. The headless latency bench ran twice on the M5 Max under a load average of 55-83 (other agents' sidecar and Chrome on the GPU) and its report rejected both runs as not a measurement (PERF.md).
- **Mac wave 1 fix (2026-11-03, `fix/wave1-b`):** S-38.3's parked Hub now reopens on its last page. `park` keeps the page's hash (`#/hub/<page>[/<section>]`, which `src/fork/hub/nav.tsx` writes with `replaceState` and WebKit reflects in the window URL) and `build_main` loads `/#/hub/…` instead of `/` (`hub::park::hub_route`; only page ids and `encodeURIComponent` characters pass; Home and `#/fork/debug` rebuild on `/`). Test `a_parked_hub_reopens_on_its_last_page`. Not verified on the Mac: queued (MAC-TEST-SCRIPT S-38.3).
- **Mac wave 2 fixes (2026-11-03, `fix/wave2`):** (1) **LLM cold start** (5,447 ms, one debug-build sample): not a Rust build question (the sidecar is the same release binary); the sample most likely included the one-time Metal library compile of a sidecar binary new to the Mac (9.6 s on a never-run copy, 1.2-2.1 s afterwards); every start also loaded the model twice (llama.cpp's `--fit on` dry run), now `--fit off` (2.0-2.4 → 1.2-1.8 s at load 13-27). No idle prewarm (S-38's memory rule; key-down already starts the sidecar). Numbers in `docs/fork/PERF.md`; release re-measure queued as S-38.6b. (2) **Test microphone** for Mac waves (`WISPRBRO_TEST_MIC_WAV`, test identities and debug builds only, never `com.wisprbro.desktop`): a file backend in the vendored recorder plays a WAV in real time through the real pipeline; the wave 2 speaker drivers use it (S-38.11). (3) Every Mac-wave test profile starts with its global shortcuts unbound and a cloned model cache (`scripts/wb-lib/profile.py`). (4) No WARN `could not bind a queued take` on ordinary takes (`bind_entry` → `.optional()`).

### Release

### S-39 1.0 release

- **Goal:** ship Wispr Bro 1.0.0 publicly.
- **User-visible outcome:** `brew install --cask <owner>/tap/wispr-bro` and a DMG on GitHub; About › Acknowledgements lists every ported source, crate, font and model with licences. S-26b's Share page shows the `brew install --cask fjenn/tap/wispr-bro` line only once `BREW_CASK_PUBLISHED` in `src/fork/hub/links.ts` is turned on: S-39 flips it in the change that publishes the cask (and fixes the tap name there if it differs).
- **R2 rows closed:** B-204, E-217
- **R4 parts:** R4 §7 ship checklist C-D; Homebrew cask (`auto_updates true`, `arch arm:/intel:` or `depends_on arch: :arm64` per DEC-29, `depends_on macos: :ventura`, `zap` under `com.wisprbro.desktop` app data incl. our models dir); `wb-tap-bump.yml` (~30 lines, PAT scoped to the tap); open-source-mac-os-apps and awesome-mac PRs (CC0 lists).
- **New fork files:** `src/fork/settings/Acknowledgements.tsx` (from `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md`), `.github/workflows/wb-tap-bump.yml`, README (product, privacy promise, install, licence, credits).
- **Tests:** `brew audit --cask --strict --online` (RT-38); install/uninstall `--zap`; RS-12 re-run 1.0.0-rc → 1.0.0.
- **Exit criteria:** §9 M1 exit checklist passes; release published (not prerelease); `latest.json` resolves.
- **Effort / depends on:** M / S-37, S-38 and the §9 checklist. Homebrew/cask self-submission later, when the repo is ≥ 30 days old with ≥ 225 stars (or 90 forks/watchers).

## 9. M1 exit criteria ("dictation is perfect")

M2 starts only when every box is ticked. Evidence goes in `docs/fork/M1-EXIT.md` with links to test runs. Intel items apply only if DEC-29 keeps Intel.

**Latency and resources**

- [ ] Every §5.1 latency row met on M1 8 GB, M-series 16 GB+ and the Intel Mac (DEC-29) (S-38 report), or the model tier was changed to meet it.
- [ ] §5.2 memory and CPU ceilings met, including the 8 h idle run with the bar visible (≤ 50 MB growth) and the combined idle RSS of all processes.
- [ ] §5.3 startup and first-run budgets met at 10 and 50 Mbit/s on clean Macs.

**Reliability**

- [ ] 500-take soak (S-38 harness) across the 10 apps of §5.4: 0 lost, 0 duplicated, 0 wrong-target insertions; every take ends in the field, on the card or in History; plus the 50-take hardware run with the same result.
- [ ] Clipboard restored byte-identical for the RS-05 type matrix; Maccy, Raycast and Paste do not record dictated text; queued delivery leaves the clipboard unchanged.
- [ ] Hotkey self-healing: RS-02 procedure passes on the release build for both taps (wake ≤ 2 s; revoke detected ≤ 2 s; re-grant recovers ≤ 5 s).
- [ ] 0 insertions in 100 fn chords, 0 orange-indicator flashes in fluent chords (key within `T_show`) and in 50 quick taps; chords keep their native function; Esc cancels in every state, and under Secure Input through the Carbon shadow (RS-14) or, if that failed, through ✕ on the pill.
- [ ] Every §5.4 negative case passes in its owning slice's test.
- [ ] No crash in the soak, the idle run or a 2-week beta with ≥ 20 users (crash files reviewed).
- [ ] Updates: 1.0.0-rc.N → rc.N+1 installs only when idle and keeps Microphone and Accessibility permissions (RS-12).

**Quality of text**

- [ ] RS-07 thresholds met by the shipped default per tier, computed per §11.1 (Light content damage ≤ 2 %, answer rate ≤ 0.5 %, filler removal ≥ 90 %, language kept ≥ 99 %; Medium damage ≤ 4 %).
- [ ] Newlines, voice commands, dictionary rules and snippets pass their acceptance tables (S-11, S-16, S-17).

**Onboarding**

- [ ] 10 testers on clean Macs (8 GB, 16 GB, one Intel if DEC-29): median completion ≤ 5 min, 90th percentile ≤ 8 min, first dictation ≤ 2 min after install and ≤ 60 s after permissions, 0 stuck (S-29b definition).

**Parity with R1** (each item ticked with a screenshot in `docs/fork/PARITY.md`; behaviour and structure match, copy is our own)

- [ ] R1 §2 Flow Bar: resting bar, active capsule with ✕/waveform/✓, mic label, fallback card, processing and error states, hover tooltip, click to start, "Hide for an hour"; three-button bar tooltips (two buttons until M2).
- [ ] R1 §3 hotkeys: every action except "Join meeting" (M2), with the panel (edit, delete, add, Reset to default, Done).
- [ ] R1 §4 Hub: Dictation home (greeting, stats rail, Voice Profile at 1,700 words, history with all row actions), Insights (both tabs), Dictionary, Snippets, Style (all tabs incl. Cleanup), Transforms, Scratchpad, notifications panel, theme Light/Dark/System. Not Notetaker, profile menu, team, referral.
- [ ] R1 §5 Settings: General, System and a local Data and Privacy page; Vibe coding. Not Notetaker, Connectors, MCP, Account, Team, Plans and Billing.
- [ ] R1 §6 and §11 menus and tray, minus meeting items; Help menu with the macOS search box.
- [ ] R1 §10 guided flows: Help popover, Transforms guide (How it works, two-step practice with the view-changes and ⌘Z hint, Polish modal), Scratchpad window, three-button bar (two buttons until M2).
- [ ] R1 §11 dialogs: Add to vocabulary (Add disabled until filled), four-step Import dictionary with the format link, Add snippet, Scratchpad shortcut, quiet-dictation guide; setup checklist card in the sidebar.
- [ ] R1 §12 quiz including the mic-choice first step and the "more about mics" link, with the recommendation table (Copy instead of Email).
- [ ] R1 §13 onboarding local variant: permissions with pictures, mic test (wired/built-in advice; list order built-in, Auto-detect, others), shortcut test, hands-free practice, first-dictation celebration, time saved starting at 0 with the typing test inside the Practice stage, first-use chooser, pill spotlight, home banner.
- [ ] Declared parity gaps (accepted, not failures): snippets are plain text (DEC-12); typing-time slider default 2 h instead of 6 h (DEC-50); no PERSONALIZE/referral stage (§2.3).

**Privacy, accessibility, localization, licence**

- [ ] 24 h egress capture (Little Snitch or `nettop`) on a used install shows only §1 hosts; `wb-egress` green on the release build; the full Mac test script passes with networking off after setup.
- [ ] §5.5 accessibility checklist passes (keyboard-only, VoiceOver checklist, contrast AA, reduced motion; axe: 0 serious/critical).
- [ ] All 26 locales complete for the `fork` namespace and `rust.*` keys; RTL checked; 10 sample-text languages human-reviewed.
- [ ] All CI guards green; THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES and Acknowledgements complete; source tarball attached to the release.
- [ ] The three owner decisions (DEC-26, DEC-29, DEC-44) are answered and applied.

**Bugs and test runs**

- [ ] No open P1 bug. P1 = data loss, wrong-target or lost insertion, hotkey dead without notice, crash, privacy breach (unlisted network call, text persisted against the rules, or the clipboard overwritten without a user action), onboarding blocker. At most 10 open P2 bugs, each with a workaround.
- [ ] The Mac test script (§11.3) passes on M1 8 GB, an M-series 16 GB+ and one Intel Mac (DEC-29), together covering macOS 13, 14, 15 and 26.

## 11. Test and release strategy

### 11.1 Automated tests and eval metrics

- **Rust unit tests** next to each fork module, using the fakes in `fork/testutil.rs` (clock, AX, pasteboard, coordinator, network). Hot logic (hotkey processor, blocking set, text pipeline, LLM guards, model policy, stats rules, retention, reconcile) targets ≥ 90 % line coverage.
- **Rust integration tests** run the fork plugin against mocked managers (no audio hardware), including the composed invoke handler and the Ready-ordering test (F-05).
- **Ported test suites** are kept as test vectors with attribution (anarlog, MacParakeet, FluidVoice, TypeWhisper, VoiceTypr, BetterWispr).
- **Cleanup eval metrics** (`eval/cleanup/README.md`, computed by `scripts/wb-eval-cleanup.ts`, no LLM judge). Each item has: the input transcript; the expected output per level; labelled **protected spans** (names, numbers, URLs, e-mails, code spans, dictionary terms); labelled **filler spans**; a **content-word list** (input words minus stopwords from stopwords-iso and minus filler spans); an `is_instruction` flag (the transcript is a question or a command); the language. Two people label every item independently; disagreements are resolved by discussion and the agreement rate is recorded.
  - **Content damage** (per item, yes/no): a protected span is missing or changed in the output (exact match after Unicode NFC; case-insensitive for words), or content-word recall against the expected output is below 0.95. Rate = damaged items / all items.
  - **Answer rate:** among `is_instruction` items, the share where the output answers or executes instead of transcribing: output word count > 1.5 × input word count, or ≥ 3 content words that appear in neither the input nor the expected output, or the output starts with an assistant marker from the Muesli list.
  - **Filler removal:** labelled filler spans absent from the output / all labelled filler spans (Light and Medium only).
  - **Language kept:** Handy `lang_id` of the output equals the labelled language, over items with ≥ 5 words.
  - **Guard catch rate:** damaging pre-guard outputs (content damage or answer) that the guard chain rejected / all damaging pre-guard outputs.
  - Gates use **post-guard** output (what the user gets); model selection in RS-07 also reports pre-guard numbers. Runs use temperature 0 and a fixed seed.
- **Nightly cleanup eval** (F-10 set) on a self-hosted Apple Silicon runner; a drop beyond RS-07 thresholds fails the nightly.
- **CI guards** (§4.12) on every PR; `wb-egress` nightly and on release.

### 11.2 Playwright (UI)

- `playwright.fork.config.ts`, mocked Tauri IPC for every fork command and event (`tests/fork/fixtures`).
- One spec per page and per pill state; light and dark; one RTL locale; reduced motion; axe checks on every spec.
- Onboarding: every step and every "No" branch, resume after relaunch, slow-download practice.
- Features that Playwright cannot prove (CSP with the real asset protocol, notifications, menus) are covered by real-build smoke runs (F-08, S-25, RS-10).

### 11.3 Manual Mac test script (outline of `docs/fork/MAC-TEST-SCRIPT.md`)

Run per release candidate on M1 8 GB, M-series 16 GB+ and Intel (DEC-29) (≈ 3 h each); results in `docs/fork/test-runs/<version>-<machine>.md`.

1. Clean install from DMG and Homebrew; Gatekeeper; permissions; onboarding timing.
2. Hotkeys: grammar table, chords (native function and no mic indicator), Esc, fallback profile, Secure Input (RS-14 cases), sleep/wake, revoke/re-grant.
3. Flow Bar: states, click, hover, drag, hide, displays (mixed scale), full screen, Spaces, top position.
4. Insertion matrix: 10 apps × (short, multi-line, snippet, no text field, password field), clipboard restore types, clipboard managers, IME, app switch during processing.
5. First run at 10 and 50 Mbit/s, offline first launch with queued takes, captive portal, language change.
6. Cleanup levels, styles per app group, AI-chat skip, context awareness, voice commands.
7. Transforms, diff, auto-apply, Command Mode on and off, ⌘Z.
8. Hub: history actions, search, dictionary, snippets, insights, scratchpad, notifications, settings, menus, tray.
9. Updates: idle install, beta channel, permissions kept.
10. Privacy: egress capture, offline run, export, delete all.
11. Accessibility: VoiceOver checklist, keyboard-only, contrast, reduced motion.
12. Soak: 100 takes and 2 h idle (full 500-take, 50-take hardware and 8 h runs before 1.0).

### 11.4 Channels

- **Internal alpha** (`1.0.0-alpha.N`) from F-02: team only, draft releases; testers update through the fork debug panel's "Check now".
- **Beta channel** from S-28: the rolling `beta` prerelease whose `latest.json` the app reads when "Receive beta updates" is on (Advanced, S-14; endpoint chosen in Rust, F-02). ≥ 20 external testers for ≥ 2 weeks before 1.0.
- **Stable**: `releases/latest/download/latest.json`.

### 11.5 Release cadence

- Before 1.0: alpha builds as slices land; betas weekly from S-28.
- After 1.0: beta weekly, stable minor every 4 weeks, patch releases for P1 bugs within 48 h. Every release runs the guards, `wb-verify`, `wb-egress`, the Mac test script (abridged for patches) and RS-12.

### 11.6 Upstream merge cadence

Weekly automated sync PR (§4.13); merged every two weeks during M1 after the hook checklist and the full test suite pass; security fixes immediately; never in the 3 days before a release. Upstream-bound fixes (e.g. the `HandyKeysState::new` error path, `llm_client` request options, coordinator `external_command`, a recorder `take_samples()` if S-06 needs it, the Moonshine licence label) are offered upstream as MIT-only PRs following Handy's PR template.

## 12. Risks and mitigations (top 15)

| #   | Risk                                                                                                                    | Mitigation                                                                                                                                                                          | Gate                              |
| --- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------- |
| 1   | macOS still runs the Globe action when fn is our hotkey, or blocking fn breaks fn chords                                | Own blocking tap with `{FN}` always blocked; RS-01 checks every "Press 🌐" value and 100 chords; DEC-44 options (pending owner) where suppression fails                             | RS-01 → S-01, S-29a               |
| 2   | The event taps die after sleep, revoke or re-sign and the user does not know                                            | Error path fix, axwatch + probe for both taps, pill/tray/notification, auto-recovery                                                                                                | RS-02 → S-02                      |
| 3   | The always-visible pill leaks webview memory or loses first clicks                                                      | Exact-size panel, no idle animation, `accept_first_mouse`, Handy's overlay never built; native resting pill as plan B                                                               | RS-03 → S-03, S-38                |
| 4   | AX gaps in Electron/Chromium cause false "no text field" cards or failed transforms                                     | Fail-open rule, lease, menu-Copy and ⌘C fallbacks, clipboard replace                                                                                                                | RS-04 → S-05, S-21                |
| 5   | Clipboard restore loses user data, clipboard managers log dictated text, or late results overwrite the clipboard        | Full snapshot, transient + concealed markers, changeCount guard, documented exclusions; queued and late results never write the clipboard                                           | RS-05 → S-04, S-07c               |
| 6   | LLM + ASR exceed memory on 8 GB Macs                                                                                    | Memory policy, ordered loads, one idle owner, smaller tier model, skip cleanup under pressure                                                                                       | RS-06 → S-10, S-38                |
| 7   | Small models change meaning or answer dictated questions                                                                | Light default, guard chain, AI-chat skip, Undo AI edit, eval gate with defined metrics                                                                                              | RS-07 → S-12a                     |
| 8   | Cleanup latency misses the budget                                                                                       | Ordered prewarm at key-down, deadline with fail-open, tier downgrade, Apple FM path                                                                                                 | RS-06, RS-08 → S-12a, S-12b, S-38 |
| 9   | The starter model gives a weak first impression                                                                         | Per-language starter choice, quick swap, "improving accuracy" copy                                                                                                                  | RS-09 → S-07a                     |
| 10  | `llama-server` fails signing, notarization, macOS 13 or the Unix socket                                                 | CI static build with 13.0 target; verify job; loopback-with-key fallback; own small server binary as last fallback                                                                  | RS-06, RS-10 → S-10               |
| 11  | Fork plumbing breaks: ACL, init order, upstream merges                                                                  | Composed handler with CI assertions, `on_ready` rule with an ordering test, listed one-line hooks with a hook-size guard, weekly sync, upstream-bound fixes sent upstream           | F-05, F-03, F-09                  |
| 12  | Updater key loss or identity change strands users or resets permissions                                                 | Key with two offline backups, identity guard, fixed identity before external builds, RS-12 every release                                                                            | F-02, F-03, RS-12                 |
| 13  | Licence contamination (GPL-2.0-only, non-free weights, missing attribution, AGPL file marking, GPL code in Handy files) | Licence register, cargo-about guard, SPDX guard (incl. upstream files), NOTICE, never-default model list, DEC-26 to the owner                                                       | F-03, F-04                        |
| 14  | Private APIs or Secure Input behaviour change on a macOS update                                                         | Feature checks, public fallback paths, re-run RS-01, RS-03 and RS-14 on each macOS beta                                                                                             | RS-01, RS-03, RS-14               |
| 15  | M1 scope (46 slices) slips and quality drops                                                                            | Strict dependency order with no forward dependencies; split slices with their own exits; only S-33 (gated by RS-13) and S-35 (optional importer) may move to 1.1 without failing §9 | §9                                |

Also tracked: trademark risk of the name (owner accepted; identity lives only in the overlay and i18n overrides, so a rename is one slice; our UI copy is our own, DEC-45); the CC-BY default (DEC-26) can be applied either way by switching the English pick table in the catalog overlay.

S-35 lists no rows by design.

---

## 15. Summary

1. Slices: M0 has 10 (F-01..F-10), M0.5 has 14 real-Mac spikes (RS-01..RS-14), M1 has 46 (S-01..S-39 with S-07, S-12, S-15, S-19, S-26 and S-29 split into lettered slices), M2 has 13 coarse slices (N-01..N-13).
2. First five to build: F-05 fork scaffolding (composed invoke handler, `on_ready`, translate script), F-07 fork settings store, F-01 identity overlay (incl. version line and Rust strings), F-06 `fork.db`, F-02 signing/notarization/updater/channels; spikes RS-01, RS-03, RS-04, RS-05, RS-07, RS-09 start in parallel on dev builds.
3. First five M1 slices: S-01 hotkey engine on the fork's own blocking tap, S-03 Flow Bar, S-02 hotkey self-healing and Secure Input, S-04 insertion and clipboard, S-05 AX layer and "no text field" card.
4. Riskiest assumption 1: macOS Globe actions can be suppressed by blocking the fn press without breaking fn chords (RS-01); where not, the owner picks a DEC-44 option.
5. Riskiest assumption 2: a 2B local model plus the ASR model fit an 8 GB Mac and clean 50 words within 2.2 s p95, also from cold with ordered loads (RS-06).
6. Riskiest assumption 3: small local models reach ≤ 2 % content damage at Light with our guards, as defined in §11.1 (RS-07).
7. Riskiest assumption 4: an always-visible webview pill stays clickable without focus loss and the whole app stays within 250 MB idle with Handy's overlay never built (RS-03).
8. Riskiest assumption 5: fail-open AX decisions avoid false "no text field" cards in Electron/Chromium apps, and a statically built `llama-server` signs and runs on macOS 13 (RS-04, RS-06, RS-10).
9. Every R2 row is placed: 325 TUNE/EXTEND/BUILD/unclassified rows map to a slice; only B-209 (lower quants), E-60 (Linux packaging) and E-210 (Windows/Linux system audio) are deferred, with reasons. Not protected in M1: HAVE row B-18 (Windows/Linux permission handling).
10. Three owner decisions are pending: DEC-26 (CC-BY-4.0 speech weights as silent defaults), DEC-44 (Globe key when suppression fails), DEC-29 (Intel support).
